- ~~**WATCH (HO 758) · the first Nov-4 read: the race pages and the band turn to results.** opened 2026-09-29; *Owner:* Code; **dated: Wednesday 2026-11-04** (`date -u -d 2026-11-04 +%A`), after 8am MT. The procedure, in order: (a), then (c), then (b), then the reads. HO 777 rehearsed (a) and (b) and read (c) in code; HO 778 built (a)'s lock and (c)'s route (their blocks in `docs/roadmap.md`). **(a) the ballot, by authorized POSTs of `/api/cron/general-ballot`** (Bearer `$CRON_SECRET`, read from `.env` by name), not a manual `npm run sync:general-ballot`, which runs from this box, walled since HO 773. Each POST is one 40-race tick from pdx1 at 6s start to start, and expires `general-ballot` itself once it reads; HO 777's (#24309) read 40 of 40 in 234.5s. The 470 races take 12 calls (eleven of 40, one of 30), about 46 minutes back to back and about 48 with a 10s pause after each run closes: the 6s spacing holds inside a run only, and since HO 778 the lock stops a second call while one runs (a call that finds another general-ballot run in flight, started inside the 300s ceiling, reads nothing and names it), so the 10s pause stays as a margin. Every call stays clear of :15-:35 of an even UTC hour (the scheduled tick) and of 00:00-00:10 and 12:00-12:10 UTC (the primaries cron's); 8am MT on Nov 4 is 15:00Z, standard time, and from then the twelve end near 15:48Z, before 16:15Z. From this box the POST's client side dropped at 60s while the run went on to close `success` (`fetch failed`, #24309), so each call's outcome is its `cron_runs` row, read before the next call, and a dropped call is never re-sent unread. A run stopped by an UNREAD leaves its races queued, so the calls go on until no race's `last_attempt_at` predates the first call. **(b) then one authorized POST of `/api/cron/race-challengers`,** whose `expireTag("races")` reaches every HO 758 reader (each carries the `races` tag), as HO 777 rehearsed it (#24312: 200 in 2.2s, 914 rows re-derived, its log line printed after the expiry). Then the pages, the band and **`/electoral`'s rows** read on Production (HO 767: `getRaceResults` carries the `races` and `general-ballot` tags, so the same POST reaches it; on LIST each decided row's chip, `{P} HOLD` / `{P} GAIN` / `{P} WIN`, and its check against its race page's *Decided* and *Elected*, a two-mark row's RUNOFF, the column head reading Result, a called seat with no Kalshi line in its open row and none on its MAP card, and the folded calendar reading all voted with no NEXT). **(c) Louisiana, by one authorized `POST /api/cron/primaries?state=LA`** (HO 778: the six House contests read live from pdx1, the House pass with `bypassCache` and its 1s pacing, no cursor and no priority pass), after the 12:00Z tick and the last ballot call, clear of :15-:35 of an even UTC hour, with its `cron_runs` row (route `/api/cron/primaries?state=LA`) read before (b); then each of the six boxes is compared with Ballotpedia. `isSettled` freezes a contest on its first winner mark, so a partial call (one advancer marked before the second) would stay a one-winner *Elected*; any such row is corrected by hand from the page. AND session = ?`) is read once per session, before the loop (`:420`; the loop starts at `:428`). Every menu roll at or below it is skipped (`:432`, `if (rollInt <= lastNum)`). A failed roll is only counted, with `stats.votesFailed++` at `:445` (the detail has no `roll_call_vote`) and at `:465` (any throw), and the loop moves on to the next roll. So once roll N fails and N+1 is written, in that run or a later one, every later run's `MAX` is above N. No code deletes a `votes` row. The watermark loop retries a failed roll only while nothing above it in its session has been written, which means it was the last roll the run attempted or every later roll failed too. The cron (`0 10 * * *`, `vercel.json:66-67`) and `npm run sync:senate-votes` (`scripts/sync-senate-votes.ts:4`) both run this same function with its defaults, and it is the only writer of Senate `votes` rows (`:219`). `fetchXml` retries network errors and 502/503 up to three times (`:124`, `:134`). Any other non-ok status, 404 and 500 included, throws on the first response (`:140-142`). **The HO 567 heal pass covers only part of it.** It selects `votes` rows with a nonzero tally and no `member_votes`, newest first (`:336-341`), capped at `HEAL_CAP = 10` (`:33`). That catches a roll that failed *after* its `votes` upsert (`:272`), in the `member_votes` batch (`:321`). It cannot see a roll that failed earlier, in the detail fetch (`:440`), the missing-`roll_call_vote` branch (`:443`), the bill lookup (`:259`) or the upsert itself, because that roll has no `votes` row. This is the complement of the HO 566/567 oddity "A windowed sync freezes its own inflow bugs" (`docs/oddities.md:754-756`): the heal pass re-visits a row written wrong on its first pass, and nothing re-visits a row that was never written. **The failure is silent.** `/api/sync-votes` returns only `{ payload }` (`app/api/sync-votes/route.ts:76`, no `chronicErr`), so `cron_runs` reads `success` (`lib/cron-log.ts:167`), and `/api/health` reads only status (`lib/cron-health.ts:124`). The count is stored at `$.payload.senate.votesFailed`, and no code reads it back: `git grep votesFailed` outside docs finds only the field declarations, the increments and the log lines at `:470` and `:483`. The failed id reaches only the runtime log (`:444`, `:464`). This is separate from the `/api/sync-votes` entry in the HO 744 `/api/health` line (an unbounded House fetch starving the Senate leg), because this one needs no hang. **The measurement that tests it, not run, is a gap in the roll sequence in `votes`, not an absence from `member_votes`.** A stranded roll has no `votes` row to join from, and "a `votes` row with no `member_votes`" is the heal pass's own set. The query is `SELECT congress, session, MAX(roll_call) - COUNT(*) AS missing FROM votes WHERE chamber = 'senate' GROUP BY congress, session`. `id` is the primary key (`scripts/migrate.ts:378`) and carries the roll (`:150`), so `COUNT(*)` counts distinct rolls. The query reads the `votes` table, which the sync itself fills, so it can only find gaps below `MAX`: it assumes Senate rolls run 1..N in each session, and it cannot see a missing roll above `MAX` (the next run fetches that one anyway). For any nonzero row, the authority is the menu XML's `vote_number` list (`:413-414`, read at `:430`). For history, read the `/api/sync-votes` rows in `cron_runs` where `json_extract(payload, '$.payload.senate.votesFailed') > 0`. *Candidate remedies, not built:* skip against the set of stored ids instead of `MAX`, so that any menu roll with no row is fetched; this also fills gaps that already exist in the sessions it syncs, and a permanent 404 costs one GET per run. Or stop the ascending loop at the first failure, so `MAX` stays below it; this stalls the session behind a permanent 404 and fills no existing gap. *Close:* both of two checks. (a) A two-run leg on a `file:` copy. First delete that session's `votes` rows from roll N up from the copy, with their `member_votes` deleted before them because `vote_id` references `votes(id)` (`scripts/migrate.ts:403`); on an unmodified copy both rolls are at or below `MAX`, and run 1 would skip them. Force roll N's detail fetch to fail in run 1 only. Run 1 fails N and writes N+1, run 2 writes N, and the copy's gap query reads 0 for that session. (b) At prod, the gap query reads 0 for every session the sync reaches (the current congress's two, `:380-381`), or every roll it reports missing is also absent from that session's menu. *Reads if never done:* on the copy, run 2 counts N as `skipped` (`:432-434`), and the gap query reads `missing ≥ 1` for that session however many runs follow. - ~~**The kalshi cron's comment says a failed chamber-control read leaves the prior blob in place, and the code overwrites that chamber with `null` on every read that fails or prices fewer than two outcomes (HO 744).** opened 2026-09-23; *Owner:* Code, **outside HO 744's scope** (filed from it), low; which half is intended, the comment or the code, is Corey's ruling (a product call, see *Why this is a ruling*), then Code. **Mechanism, read in code at `252411f`, not measured:** `app/api/cron/kalshi/route.ts:74` says *"Non-fatal: a failed read just leaves the prior blob in place."* `fetchChamberControl` turns each chamber's failure into `null` in two ways: a thrown read is caught by `.catch(() => null)` (`lib/kalshi.ts:211-212`; `kalshiGet` throws on a non-OK status at `:95` and on exhausted 429 retries at `:98`, and aborts each attempt at 20s at `:90`), and a read that prices fewer than two outcomes returns `null` at `:198`. The route then upserts `JSON.stringify(control)` unconditionally (`route.ts:75-81`, `ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value = excluded.value, updated_at = excluded.updated_at`). So the *non-fatal* half is true only in that neither catch lets `fetchChamberControl` reject (a slow read can still carry the tick past the 55s cap, which is the `/api/health` `timeout` line's question, not this one's), and the *prior blob* half is false. The row becomes `{"house":null,…}`: a JSON null on that chamber, not a SQL NULL, with `updated_at` advanced. Because each chamber has its own catch, a failure on one chamber nulls that chamber while the other updates, and the helper's own comment, written in the same commit, already says so (`lib/kalshi.ts:207-208`, *"the per-chamber catch keeps one bad read from nulling the other"*). `expireTag("races")` (`route.ts:124`) then flushes `getChamberControl` (`lib/queries.ts:3086-3103`, the blob's only reader), and `/electoral`'s hero band renders `—` for that chamber (`components/RacesHeroBand.tsx:18-21`), in the HOUSE case beside its `LIVE` pill (`:60`), until the next tick that prices it (`15 */2 * * *`, `vercel.json:106-107`). The route comment and the code have disagreed since they were written together at `976478a` (HO 219), so this is not a regression. **Why this is a ruling and not a fix:** making the code match the comment means merging each chamber with the stored blob, and the band shows no timestamp (`.claude/skills/cbt/SKILL.md:1879`, *"no timestamp, decided against"*). A chamber that stops pricing would then show its last pct with no mark that it is stale, and for the House that pct would sit beside the band's only liveness signal, its `LIVE` pill. Making the comment match the code is a comment edit, and the HO 219 helper comment above already reads that way. **The measurement that would show how often it has happened, not run:** each tick's `control.house` and `control.senate` are part of the success payload (`route.ts:129-130`) and are stored in `cron_runs.payload` (`lib/cron-log.ts:166-167`, `:88`), so `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM cron_runs WHERE route = '/api/cron/kalshi' AND status = 'success' AND (json_type(payload,'$.payload.house') = 'null' OR json_type(payload,'$.payload.senate') = 'null')` counts the success ticks that wrote a null chamber, and the matching rows' `started_at` gives the date of each. Zero is a floor, not "never shown": a `timeout` row stores no payload (`lib/cron-log.ts:177-183`), yet the handler it raced is not cancelled (`:163`) and may still reach the upsert; and a viewer with odds off sees neither KALSHI cell (`html[data-odds="off"] .odds-only`, `app/globals.css:3240`). *Close:* the comment and the code agree, in one of two ways. (2) The write keeps a chamber's prior value when its read returns `null`. This is proved by one tick on a local production build against a `file:` copy seeded with sentinel House pcts that no market prints, with only the `CONTROLH-2026` read made to fail by a `NODE_OPTIONS=--require` fetch shim (the HO 744 legs driver's method, `scripts/diagnostic/ratings-hang-legs-744.ts`), then reading the sentinel back for the House while the Senate updates live. *Reads if never done:* `route.ts:74` still claims the prior blob is kept, and the one-chamber-failure tick reads `"house":null` with the Senate updated. - ~~**Deployments older than the HO 680 redeploy still carry the burned `CRON_SECRET` (opened 2026-09-19).** opened 2026-09-19; *Owner:* **Corey** for the deletions or the protection toggle (dashboard-side, and destructive in the first case); **Code** for the reads. **Anchored by name, not by line number** — see *Backlog and roadmap `:N` self-references are as-of markers* below. **The premise, stated as a claim rather than a measurement.** A deployment's env is **frozen at build time** — the mechanism the **HO 680 `CONGRESS_API_KEY` strike** already records, which is why removing a value from the project does not reach builds that already hold it. So the **HO 680 `CRON_SECRET` strike**'s read-back, *rejected by production*, is true **from the HO 680 redeploy onward** (`dpl_EpDeMWgRYht22urXJB1MBxnB3BYN` → `congressionalterminal-2jn5uykh6`, aliased to the production host at `a3b7134`) and **is not true of older URLs** — previews and superseded production builds alike, each still holding the value HO 678 ruled burned. **Preview protection is off** (**HO 703's preview-protection reading**, measured), so those URLs answer without a Vercel auth wall. **And each such build also holds the never-rotated `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`** (**the HO 678 `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN` WATCH**: `EdDSA`, **no `exp` claim at all**, so it ends only by explicit invalidation). GSA's deletion of the old Congress key (**the GSA WATCH struck 2026-09-19**) killed the Congress half of this and **does not touch the secret half**. *Close — any of three:* **(a)** the deployments are deleted; **(b) Vercel Deployment Protection (Standard)**, which covers preview URLs and superseded production URLs together and leaves only the production domain open — **one dashboard toggle rather than a sweep of builds, and the likeliest route**; or **(c)** a tombstone carrying Corey's reason for accepting the exposure — an accepted risk recorded is a close, an unrecorded one is not. *Instrument, the same shape for (a) and (b):* a before-read of **200** on `/api/version`, then **non-200** — **401** where protection is on, **404** where the deployment is gone. **The before-read is the control, and 200 is exactly what the instrument reads if nothing was done**, so the non-200 means something only against a 200 taken first. *Stated as inference, not reading:* **that superseded PRODUCTION builds are publicly reachable is inferred from HO 703's preview-protection reading, not measured** — protection being off is a project setting, and whether an unaliased production build answers on its own URL is a separate question. **The first before-read settles it**, and if those URLs 404 already the line narrows to previews rather than closing.~~ · **CLOSED 2026-09-19 ON THE PROTECTION ROUTE — route (b), the cheap one, and the reads bracket it from both sides.** Corey enabled **Vercel Authentication (Standard Protection)**, which covers preview URLs and superseded production URLs together and leaves only the production domain open. *The before is HO 703's, not today's* — protection went on during the previous session's fast-forward, so no 200 was read against an old URL today and this strike does not pretend one was. HO 703 measured it, verbatim: **"two Preview URLs returned 200 to a session-less request"**. *The after, taken 2026-09-19T21:31:49Z:* `dpl_8aW1UZaLx3XEQJVZ1Ccs5TmpdyXV` (preview, 678-review `af55304`, pre-rotation) → **302** to `vercel.com/sso-api`; `dpl_9nUYhEL5HnE8SytQfNHhFaopWewn` (**superseded production**, main `842a705`, older than the redeploy) → **302** to `vercel.com/sso-api`; the production domain → **200** serving `c95d27f`. Captures in `docs/handoffs/739-artifacts/after-read.txt`. **THE WALL IS A 302, NOT A 401, AND THAT IS THE ONE THING NOBODY PREDICTED.** Both this line and the HO 703 comment expected a 401. Vercel answers an SSO **redirect**, and `curl -f` fails on 4xx/5xx and **not** on 3xx — so the first CI run after the toggle did not fail at its fetch at all: it passed the redirect body to `JSON.parse` and died with `SyntaxError: Unexpected token 'R'`, a message naming nothing about protection. The instrument in this line's own *Close* clause said **non-200**, which was right and is why the reading still worked; a 401-shaped check would have been the thing that missed. **The line's stated inference is settled, and it was right to flag it as one.** It said *that superseded PRODUCTION builds are publicly reachable is inferred from HO 703's preview-protection reading, not measured*. Measured now: the superseded production URL was **reachable** (it answers, it is not a 404) and is **now walled**. So the line closes on protection rather than narrowing to previews — the narrowing branch its text provided for did not fire. **The GitHub-secret clause closes with it.** A dormant copy of the burned `CRON_SECRET` sat in GitHub Actions secrets, last updated **2026-06-02** — three months before HO 680 rotated the value — **referenced by no workflow**, found by STEP 0 rather than looked for. Deleted by Corey 2026-09-19; `gh secret list` is the read-back and shows it absent at every scope (repo, org — 422, the owner is a user account — and both environments empty). *Same class, different store:* the burned value in a durable one. **What the toggle cost, and how it was paid.** Protection walls Preview URLs, and `narrow-preview`'s whole target is a Preview, so the width gate went red minutes after the save. Paid by `x-vercel-protection-bypass` at **both** fetch sites — the `/api/version` curl and Playwright via `playwright.config.ts`'s `use.extraHTTPHeaders`, one site covering every `page.goto` in every spec. **The bypass value is not new: it has existed on the Vercel project since 2026-05-12 and CI is its first consumer**, added as a repository secret 2026-09-19T23:21:54Z. **The prod-domain `smoke` job is deliberately NOT given it**, and `npm run verify:deploy` and the daily crawl both hit the domain, which is unprotected — neither is affected by the toggle.

it rests on SKILL `:2511`’s coupling fact (the root program is what `next build` type-checks, so shape (A) would make a crawl-spec typo fail the Vercel Production build) and **not** on any probe failure — the interaction probe was named as the reading that could overturn it and **did not fire**. *Also owed to 709:* the `1440-reduced` third can be re-taken for real against `cbt-705-n15` / `cbt-705-n16`, **whose window closes when `backlog:51` closes on its week** — 709 takes that run or declines it in writing.~~ — **CLOSED HO 709 under shape (B′) (`e831d53` code · `7793ecd` comment).** `tsconfig.e2e.json` at the repo root extends `tsconfig.json`, sets `incremental: false` (the inherited `true` would write a `tsconfig.e2e.tsbuildinfo` that `.gitignore:9` does not cover), and replaces `include` with `["e2e/**/*.ts", "playwright.config.ts"]`; `"typecheck": "tsc --noEmit && npm run typecheck:e2e"` chains it, so `ci.yml:56` did not change and the pre-push habit is one command still. **The gate was proven able to fail before it was allowed to pass, on the real error rather than a bait:** run after the two configs and BEFORE the spec was touched, the root program was green and the second was red at exit 2 — `e2e/bundler-captures-706.spec.ts(67,9): error TS2353: Object literal may only specify known properties, and 'reducedMotion' does not exist in type 'Fixtures<{}, {}, PlaywrightTestArgs & PlaywrightTestOptions, PlaywrightWorkerArgs & PlaywrightWorkerOptions>'.` After the fix both programs read **exit 0** (each `tsc` run standalone and printed: root 0, e2e 0). **Coverage, the two reads that strike this line:** `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.e2e.json --listFiles | grep -c 'e2e[\/]smoke.spec.ts'` → **1** (183 files in the program); `tsc --noEmit --listFiles | grep -c '[\/]next.config.ts'` → **1**, root program 1,304 → **1,305 files, exactly the one addition**. The root program's `smoke.spec.ts` count is still **0 and that is (B′) working, not a residual gap** — SKILL `:2511`: the root program is what `next build` type-checks, so shape (A) would make a crawl-spec typo fail the Vercel Production build. `ci.yml:11-13` corrected in its own `ci` commit so the justification and the reality moved together, comment-only proven mechanically (8 changed lines, 0 failing `^[+-][[:space:]]*#`, counter fires 1 on a synthetic non-comment line). **The `1440-reduced` third was RE-TAKEN, not declined** (n15 15.5.15 webpack on `:3000`, n16 16.3.4 turbopack on `:3007`, both from their own builds, twenty tag flushes across the two servers all HTTP 200): control `search @1440` read `RM=false expected=false`, then baseline **36 written / 36 passed / `RM=true expected=true` 36** and compare **36 passed, exit 0, `RM=true expected=true` 36, `RM=false` 0**. **So HO 706's conclusion survives the re-take:** the reduced-motion third now genuinely emulates and n16 still matches n15 on all 36. The one-shot `npm run summarize` drained the standing backlog **1,374 → 7** (the 7 are 15s per-bill timeouts, deferred 24h → retry next tick). `summary IS NULL` now trends down, and the **cron self-heals at cap 200 / C=5 against ~30/day inflow** (~80–120/tick clears a day's inflow with room to spare) — a manual drain is no longer the only thing that lowers the count. **Foundation's roadmap 96 hold is no longer fused on this cron:** the growing summarize backlog was the last correctness drag tying Foundation to this cron's health; with the read bounded and the cron self-healing, the score no longer depends on it. See DONE → HO 406.

- ~~**`/lobbying` VOLUME sort — PROMOTED WATCH -> OPEN LOOP (HO 544; promoted HO 596)**~~ — **SHIPPED HO 597 (`82eaa24` guard split · `daf6dcc` migration · `387c56f` writer · `f2f4915` rewire · `418e80c` falsification), live on prod at `418e80c`, prod-verified. CLOSED FOR THE PATH IT NAMED; two residuals split out as their own entries below, one of them NEW and worse.** **Two defects, not one.** (1) *The one this entry named:* VOLUME ordered by a request-time `COUNT(*) FROM lda_activities GROUP BY filing_uuid`. Materialized to `lda_filings.activity_count` (+ `idx_lda_filings_activity(activity_count DESC, dt_posted DESC)`, forced via `INDEXED BY`), so the subquery and the JOIN are gone and it is a pre-ordered single-table walk. (2) *Found while fixing (1), and it is why the degradation lasted:* **the HO 440/448 guard sat INSIDE the `unstable_cache` callback, so its degraded `{items:[],…}` was the callback's RETURN VALUE and was CACHED FOR AN HOUR** — one cold miss poisoned that key for 60 minutes and the retry that would have succeeded never ran (the HO 552 class: a transient failure persisted by an exit path that records instead of deferring). The cached function now throws and the guard catches in an exported wrapper; the guard is NOT stripped. **FALSIFIED FOUR-SIDED.** *RED, co-located pdx1, shipped LIMIT 13:* the old shape breaches EVERY VOLUME variant and **does not finish in 55 SECONDS** (55002ms / 55001ms at a 55s ceiling) — worse than STEP 0 could see, because **every `20.00Xs` figure in this arc is the INSTRUMENT'S CEILING, not a measurement**: `lib/db.ts` aborts at 10s and retries once, so any query slower than 10s reports ~20.00s whether it takes 21 seconds or five minutes. STEP 0's RED is therefore **>20s (abort ceiling)**, and the true cost measured here is **>55s co-located** (itself a 55s ceiling). The data is RIGHT-CENSORED at the bound — a figure at the ceiling is a lower bound, only figures under it are durations. *GREEN, same conditions, worst of 3-5 cold runs:* volume **564ms (+9.44s margin)** · volume page-40 **1.48s (+8.52s)** · volume+linked **2.97s (+7.03s)**. *CONTROL* (`scripts/diagnostic/lda-volume-falsify-597.ts` — runs the OLD and NEW SQL against the SAME live state and diffs them, not a temporal before/after, because the corpus grows daily): the visible range **1,000 rows IDENTICAL position-for-position**, the clamp boundary identical in three forms incl. the shipped real LIMIT/OFFSET, the bill-linked branch identical, `SUM(activity_count)` == `COUNT(lda_activities)` exactly, **0 of 429 zero-activity filings visible** (rank-1000 `activity_count` = **11**, they carry 0), and the boundary key is unique so the visible set is fully determined. *DEGRADATION PATH FIRED:* matched RED/GREEN pair on one cheap cache key with one injected failure — RED served the stored empty on hits 2-3 in <600ms without touching the DB, GREEN re-queried and recovered 13 rows; the guard fired exactly ONCE in both, so the caching was the difference, not the instrument. **The comment that justified the join shape said "~295" zero-activity filings; measured 421 at STEP 0 and 429 now** — fixed in the rewire commit, since a stale number in a comment justifying a query shape is how this family gets through. (I then made the same error one line over — "~421k rows" for the scan size, conflating the zero-filing count with a row count; corrected to the measured 278,095 in `4051f9b`.) **The writer is proven end-to-end, not just by backfill:** a live `sync:lda` after the deploy ingested 63 filings / 97 activities through the new upsert, after which `SUM(activity_count)` 278,192 == `COUNT(lda_activities)` 278,192, **0 NULLs across 129,401 filings**, and 0 mismatches in the newest 80 rows. **Original WATCH text and the HO 596/597 correction history are preserved verbatim in git** (`git show d7fa0c2:docs/backlog.md` and `f190c38`) rather than re-copied here; the load-bearing part of the correction survives above, namely that "degradation, not a crash" was an accurate description of a working guard and it was the COST ESTIMATE that measurement contradicted.
- ~~**Query-cost VARIANCE — the 2-15× run-to-run spread on byte-identical work; PROMOTED to its own loop (opened HO 599, the residual of HO 598).**~~ **CLOSED by the Aug 7 platform read — the DB tier is EXONERATED and the spread was CLIENT-SIDE PATH; the close paragraph is at the end of this entry, and the body below is kept because it records what was believed on the way.** *Owner:* Corey (the platform read) / Code (the re-measure). **Three HOs in a row were decided by this rather than by the query each was scoped around**, which is why it stops riding inside them: **594** (a covering index reached 140ms-1.5s steady state and the first touch after a >60s gap still cost 8.2-14.1s), **597** (`?sort=volume&linked=1&page=40` improved hugely and still breached **2 of 5** cold runs, worst 15.62s), **598** (the entire residual of `/lobbying?q=` is leg 1 — a ~33,620-row / **~1 MB** covering scan ranging **0.3-6.6s**, at ~1/23 the bytes of the walk it replaced, carrying the same spread). **The bytes model buys the median and does not touch the variance** (oddities), and there is very little data left to remove on this path — so more query design is not the lever. **The standing explanation is sub-60s page residency on a remote store, and it has never been probed on its own terms:** it was inferred from HO 594's `?order=` / `?warm=1` controls, which pinned *that* cold spike to pages that aren't there but say nothing about why byte-identical work varies 2-15× run to run. **Distinct from the read-budget WATCH below** — that asks *who is spending rows*, this asks *why identical work takes 2-15× longer on some runs*; they share a blocker, not a question. *Blocked on a platform read only Corey can run:* `turso org usage`, the plan tier, the **primary region against the Vercel function region**, and whether the DB carries replicas (the `turso` CLI is not installed in this environment and `.env` holds only the DB `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`, no platform API token — the same block that skipped HO 582's M4b). *Close:* the spread is **explained** (a named mechanism, with those platform facts on the record) or **bounded** (a co-located worst case with the margin stated as a number) — and, concretely, a sparse `/lobbying?q=` clears the 10s bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail, which is also the close criterion of the search loop above; the two close together or neither does. *Instrument:* re-run `scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts` co-located and report **worst − median per regime**, never a range (oddities: gate on the upper tail, since a fast outlier is evidence of safety). A run that reads a materially narrower spread than 2-15× on identical work is itself the finding — it would mean the variance is conditional on something this arc never isolated.  **HO 602 — THE PLATFORM READ IS IN, AND IT KILLS TWO HYPOTHESES.** *(Turso dashboard, org `csu-j3`, read 2026-08-05; no script, not reproducible from the repo.)* Plan **Developer** ($5.99/mo, **2.5B rows read/mo**, +$1/B overage). DB primary **`aws-us-west-2`** (`TURSO_DATABASE_URL`, by name), single group `default`, **no replicas, no branches**; Vercel functions pinned **`pdx1`** (`vercel.json:2`) — us-west-2. **Store and compute are in the same AWS region, so cross-region latency and replica routing are both DEAD.** **The standing candidate is Developer-tier multi-tenancy** (Turso's own launch post describes the tier as a massive multi-tenant server) — the shape that leaves a median alone while fanning an upper tail, and consistent with the sub-60s page-residency story rather than replacing it. **There is no purchasable lever:** the plan comparison table carries quota, retention and governance rows and **no performance row at any tier**, so a Scaler upgrade is an **unfalsifiable experiment** and was rejected rather than run. **This loop is therefore now committed to closing by BOUNDING, not by explaining** — a co-located worst case with the margin stated as a number — which was always the second half of its own criterion; the *explained* half is no longer reachable with the levers available. **The blocker is discharged: do not re-file this as blocked on a platform read.** *Instrument:* unchanged (`scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts`, **worst - median per regime**).  **CLOSED — THE AUG 7 PLATFORM READ, AND IT CLOSED BY *EXPLAINING*, WHICH THIS ENTRY HAD GIVEN UP ON.** *(Turso Analytics + Vercel dashboards, read 2026-08-07; no script, not reproducible from the repo — same standing as the HO 602 read.)* **(1) THE DB TIER IS EXONERATED.** Turso's own **p50 / p95 / p99 run FLAT from Jul 18 → Aug 7** — the entire window in which the 2-15× spread was observed. A store whose percentiles do not move cannot be the source of a 2-15× fan on the caller's clock, so **Developer-tier multi-tenancy — the standing candidate this entry ADOPTED at HO 602 — is dead**, alongside the cross-region and replica hypotheses HO 602 had already killed. **(2) THE SPREAD WAS CLIENT-SIDE PATH, and the instrument was measuring itself.** Every run that produced the 2-15× figures fired **from the Windows box** — home route plus a fresh per-run TLS handshake to a US region — a cost path **prod does not have and never had**, and one that is **structurally invisible to the store's percentiles because it is spent before the query arrives**. *The numbers that opened this loop were measurements of the LINK, attributed to the QUERY.* **(3) The Jul 8-14 latency band predates tracking** — visible at the left edge, no instrumentation behind it, **unexplained and deliberately not chased**; recorded so its absence from this paragraph is not read as absence from the chart. **(4) TOPOLOGY, HEADER-VERIFIED rather than config-inferred:** `curl -sI …/api/version` returns `X-Vercel-Id: sfo1::pdx1::…`, so the **executing function region is `pdx1`** (`vercel.json:2`) against the Turso primary **`aws-us-west-2`** in the DB hostname — **HO 602's co-location claim STANDS and is now measured rather than inferred.** Fluid on, Standard CPU. **The near-miss is recorded because it is the lesson:** this session's dashboard read reported the function region as **`iad1` (Overridden)** — which would have put compute cross-country from its store and made HO 602's claim FALSE — and it was **caught by repo cross-check before it landed**; the Overridden badge marks the **INERT dashboard default**, config-as-code wins. ***A dashboard panel is a claim; `vercel.json` and a hostname are the record; a response header beats both.*** **(5) STANDING RULE:** do **not** reopen without new **PROD-SIDE** evidence — a co-located run, a runtime log, a response header. **A slow diagnostic run from the dev box is now a KNOWN NON-SIGNAL.** **AND THE SEARCH LOOP ABOVE DOES *NOT* CLOSE WITH IT, though this entry said the two close together or neither does.** That clause is honoured by naming what actually changed: `/lobbying?q=`'s criterion is **a co-located sparse run clearing the bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail**, and **no such run has been made** — what closes here is only that its residual is **no longer owned by an unexplained variance thread**, and that the re-measure it still needs must be **prod-side/co-located**, because a dev-box re-run cannot clear a bound it was never measuring. *Criterion, honestly scored:* the entry had committed to closing by **bounding**, judging the *explained* half unreachable with the levers available — **it closed by explaining anyway, from a lever it never considered: measuring the instrument's own path instead of the query's.**

This is the `<700` thread SKILL already carries as deliberately un-specced, now with a number and a named element rather than a general note. *Close:* either give the roster row a narrow-width treatment (the `.mc-*` two-pane stacks at 720 but the numeral columns do not), or state a keep reason and widen one gate to 430 so the figure stops being invisible.
- ~~**The one deploy-blocking width gate cannot read a change before it ships (opened HO 698).**~~ — **CLOSED HO 703.** *Closed by:* the `narrow-preview` job in `e2e-prod.yml`, gated to a successful **Preview** `deployment_status`, running `e2e/narrow.spec.ts` against that deployment's own `environment_url` at `--retries=0`. **The instrument that closed it is a run, not a design:** Preview run **`34269032711`** read a ref and went **RED** on exactly the planted route at both widths (`466/430 over=36`, `466/390 over=76`, over-edge `span w=450 ws=nowrap minw=450px`), and **`34269536022`** on the very next commit read **GREEN** on 36/36 routes at both — a real CI width reading on a ref, before any FF, which is precisely what this entry said could not happen. Neither of the two filed remedy candidates was needed as written: **preview protection is OFF** (measured — two Preview URLs returned 200 to a session-less request), so no bypass secret; and no local production build, so `ci.yml` stays secretless. **Two things recorded here because they will otherwise be re-derived:** Vercel sets `deployment.ref` to the **full SHA, never a branch name** (8 of 8 deployments, `payload` empty), so the proposed job-level key `e2e-preview-<deployment.ref>` would be per-commit and cancel nothing — **and a job-level key could not have worked in any form**, because `concurrency` is workflow-level and governs the whole run. **The ruled fix is a FOURTH COMPONENT in the workflow key**, `${{ … environment == 'Preview' && deployment.sha || 'na' }}`: until now every Preview-success deploy resolved to one string regardless of commit, which was harmless only while the class was **fully skipped** and is HO 685's own failure mode once `narrow-preview` makes it do work. Production bursts still collapse on `'na'`; Preview runs are per-SHA and never collapse. **Proven behaviourally:** three Preview deploys of different SHAs pushed within 18s all ran to completion (`34271627877` / `34271643386` / `34271658004`, groups differing only in the SHA), where the old key would have cancelled two. **The job also proves its target before trusting any assertion** — a `/api/version` pre-step requiring the served sha to equal the deployment's, because an SSO wall, a 401 or a stale alias all yield a page with no horizontal overflow — and the spec asserts per-route **status 2xx and landed path**, since a Preview runs the narrow spec with no smoke crawl beside it. Both fired on demand: `34271658004` red at the pre-step with the gate **skipped**, `34271643386` red on a planted 404 with the pre-step **passing**. `docs/method.md` § Environment amended in the same HO — the *before an FF there is none* clause is now false for width gates specifically and stands for everything else. *Owner:* Code. `e2e-prod.yml` is armed on `deployment_status`, which fires for **Production** only; a review ref pushes a **Preview**, so every pre-FF run of the narrow gate is SKIPPED and the first CI reading of a change arrives strictly after `main` has moved. HO 697's FF go asked for the CI reading, got a Windows local run, and `/welcome` @430 went red on the Ubuntu runner four hours later — the gap is structural, not a lapse, and it sat on the architect's side of the relay as much as Code's. **Two remedy candidates, neither built:** (a) a `workflow_dispatch` input pointing the crawl at a Preview URL — works only if Vercel deployment protection admits the runner, which is unprobed; (b) a pre-FF `narrow` run in `ci.yml` on the Ubuntu runner against a **local production build** of the review ref — needs no prod and no protection bypass, and the host is the entire point. *Close:* one of the two lands and a review ref produces a real 430 reading on Ubuntu before its FF, **or** a keep reason is recorded and `docs/method.md` § Environment's new clause stands alone as the mitigation. First instance: this red.

The gate then ran at both widths on a ref: `34269536022` green 36/36 at 430 and 390. **The order is the durable part** — a gate that reds on day one for pre-existing reasons teaches everyone to ignore it, so a candidate width is measured through the `narrow_widths` dispatch input first and promoted to the default only after. **And this entry's own closing observation held:** the two widths are not independent, and 390 found nothing precisely because HO 698/699 fixed metric-INDEPENDENTLY rather than tuning to 430. A malformed `NARROW_WIDTHS` now throws rather than silently measuring one width while reporting two. *Owner:* Code. `e2e/narrow.spec.ts` asserts the doc-scroll pair at **430 and nothing else**, so the reach gap HO 694 closed at the wide end it re-opened at the narrow one: measuring the HO 698 fix found the OLD `/welcome` CTA scrolling to **421 at 390** on the Windows box — broken on every host, at a width narrower than the single one anybody had ever measured, and invisible to every instrument in the repo. That defect is now fixed as a side effect, which is precisely why the gap needs its own line: nothing would have caught the next one. *Close:* a second width on the same spec (**390** is the candidate — it is a real phone and it is where this one showed) with its zero stated at the time it is added, **or** a reasoned reason not to — a second width doubles the routes crawled on a deploy-blocking gate, and that cost is the argument against, not an oversight. Note the two candidate widths are not independent: a fix that is metric-INDEPENDENT passes both by construction, so the second width mainly catches layouts that were tuned rather than freed.

- ~~**`#418` remedy (C) — a RESHAPED boundary, behind (b)** — opened 2026-09-08 (HO 704); *Owner:* Corey/design. Corey's verdict was *"B and then C"*, and the *then* is sequencing: (b) first. **Two shapes, both needing a mock:** the masthead lifted into `app/layout.tsx` with the boundary around the body, or a **designed skeleton** as the root boundary's fallback. **Neither buys back the body's paint time** — a server-suspending boundary makes whatever it encloses atomic, so the body still arrives at transfer-complete and all a reshape buys is the chrome arriving early. That is the honest framing of the trade, and it is why this is design-owned rather than a build. **The bar it is measured against is the HO 704 throttle tables**, re-run with `scripts/diagnostic/throttled-fcp-704.ts` plus the client-transition half `scripts/diagnostic/suspense-nav-704.ts`; the standing rule is SKILL's — no server-suspending boundary above the page segment without a designed fallback AND the throttled FCP reading in the paste. *Close:* a ruled mock plus both readings, or an explicit decline recorded here. **HO 705 — (b) IS GONE AND THIS DID NOT INHERIT ITS WORK.** (b) was superseded by an upstream fix rather than failing, so the *"B and then C"* sequencing is discharged, not skipped. So the FCP cost this entry existed to weigh — a server-suspending boundary making the body atomic, measured at HO 704 on `/members` at 1500 kbit/s as **FCP 300ms unfixed → 2,880ms fixed**, a 2,560ms blank window against a 2,394ms transfer floor (`docs/roadmap.md:1526-1527`) — **is never paid**, and no mock is owed. **The SKILL rule it cited stands on its own** and is not struck with it: no server-suspending boundary above the page segment without a designed fallback AND a throttled FCP reading in the paste. `scripts/diagnostic/throttled-fcp-704.ts` and `suspense-nav-704.ts` remain the instruments if a boundary is ever proposed for another reason.

The clause above demands *"12px uniform"*, which was written for the THIRD escape — the one that removed the pixel by narrowing the ruled figure and moving the wrap point; this one keeps the figure at **13px**, a case that criterion cannot grade. **The mechanism (`ed6df20`, `app/globals.css:771` / `:859` / `:897` / `:900`):** `column-gap: 0 → 1px` on `.primary-nav`, `border-left: 1px solid → none` plus `box-shadow: -1px 0 0 var(--border-soft)` on `.pnav-item + .pnav-item`, the divider's margins `0 4px → 0 3px` and its reset `border-left: none → box-shadow: none`. A border takes 1px of LAYOUT on every adjacent sibling and CSS cannot see a wrap boundary; a `column-gap` applies between items on the same line only and never at the start of a wrapped line, and an outset shadow takes no layout at all — so the gap carries the space and the shadow carries the ink. **Measured both ways on a local production build, `/` and `/bills` at 1440 and 2560 (`nav-geometry-690.ts`):** label-to-label **13px uniform → 13px uniform**; row-2 first label `/`@1440 **+1px → 0px**; divider **21px → 21px**; masthead **0px → 0px**; row-1 item count at 1440 **8 → 8**; active-vs-inactive **0px → 0px**. Both surviving marks are written into the `:809-858` comment as accepted drawing. The mock's § 02 caption stays *"2. 6px"* and carries a dated HO 735 notes line recording that the shipped mechanism is no longer its `border-left`. Captures both ways in `docs/handoffs/735-artifacts/` (local-only).

The nearest thing to a companion is `Identical bill`, and the numbers that decide the ruling are these: it covers **3,237 bills**, **89.3% of its rows are cross-chamber** (3,083 of 3,454; 371 are same-chamber), and **69 bills carry MORE THAN ONE `Identical bill`** — so "promote *the* companion" has no unique referent on those. The choice, stated as the choice it is: **(a)** promote `Identical bill` as-is and accept that 10.7% of promotions are same-chamber and 69 bills promote an arbitrary one of several; **(b)** promote `Identical bill` **only where the chamber differs**, which is the strictest reading of "companion" and covers 3,083 rows, leaving same-chamber identicals in the ordinary list; **(c)** something else. **Whichever is chosen must also decide `Identical Bill (Became Law)`** — 24 rows, and note the **capital B**, so a `WHERE relationship_type = 'Identical bill'` silently excludes them (oddities). *Close:* the UI HO cannot be written until this is answered; it is not a Code decision and HO 674 deliberately did not make it.

Decomposed at close: roster-ahead **1,901** · count-ahead **4** · no-roster **0** · agree **12,026**, of 13,931 carrying a count. **The finding, which outlives the fix:** HO 674 STEP 0.4's `active roster <> cosponsor_count` selects 1,910 bills of which **1,906 are ones where the ROSTER is the fresher side** — it never clears, because the stale half is a column this HO may not correct. Adding DIRECTION took it to 10 + 73; the STEP 3 slice then caught direction alone re-firing forever on **5 bills whose comparand is stale ABOVE the truth** (four where a withdrawal dropped `pagination.count` while the stored column kept the old value, one where `$.relatedBills.count` reads 1 against an API returning 0), so MOVEMENT (`count <> count_at_check`) was added and falsified on the same rows, **5 → 0**. **A trigger compared against a value you are not allowed to correct must fire on that value MOVING, not on it disagreeing with you.** *Residual, stated so it is not read as complete:* **510 of 17,729** bills were re-checked in the shipping session; a full sweep cycle is **~18.5 days** at 8 ticks/day × 120 bills (~1,920 requests/day, 2 per bill — the refresh always fetches both rosters, because gating the related fetch on a possibly-stale count would reintroduce the blindness the sweep exists to backstop). Meanwhile `/api/sync` runs `0 */6 * * *` (**4×/day**) and adds bills. **Two consequences, and the second is the one that keeps growing:** a bill introduced from now on renders an empty Cosponsors row and no related-bills block **permanently**, and every existing roster is frozen at its backfill reading while cosponsors keep accruing — which is the same mechanism as the `cosponsor_count` entry below, now pointed at the table that was supposed to be the fix. **The decay is measured, not modelled, and it was already measurable hours after the backfill landed:** the backfill's last write is `2026-08-20T01:02:19.943Z`, and **5 bills carry `cosponsor_count > 0` with ZERO roster rows** (of 13,929 bills carrying a count, corpus 17,728). One of them is unambiguous — **`119-s-5354`, count 6, upserted `2026-08-20T03:28:43Z`**, i.e. after the backfill's last write, so a bill has already arrived with no roster. The other four (`119-s-5319` · `119-s-5311` · `119-s-5310` · `119-hres-1400`) are **not characterised** — backfill miss or the same class — and telling them apart is the sync HO's first question, not a claim made here. **The trigger does not need designing: HO 674's STEP 0.4 already worked it out** — **active roster size** (`sponsorship_withdrawn_date IS NULL`) **against `bills.cosponsor_count`**, which is a free integrity check because both are already stored. **The withdrawal trap is load-bearing on it:** `pagination.count` excludes withdrawals while the list includes them, so a comparison that does not filter to active disagrees on exactly the bills that have one (77 rows corpus-wide). - ~~**`bills.cosponsor_count` is systematically stale — 13.8% of bills, and always low (opened HO 674)**~~ — **CLOSED at HO 677 (`061f96d`) by giving the column an OWNER, which is the question the entry was really asking.** Of its three acceptable outcomes it took the first: the count is **derived**, written by `lib/bill-rosters-refresh.ts` from the same `pagination.count` the HO 676 watermark caches, so the column and `bill_roster_state.active_count` cannot drift apart by construction. **`/api/sync` no longer writes it** — omitted from both the INSERT list and the `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET`, with the omission stated there (the HO 459 rule, which exists because an unexplained omission gets helpfully re-added). **The close criterion, re-measured as it demanded and stated as a fraction:** disagreement **1,905 of 13,931 → 0 of 13,931**, and NULL-despite-a-roster **108 → 0**. A one-shot reconcile shipped in the same commit because ownership alone cannot move a number when only 510 of 17,729 bills have been refresh-checked — 2,013 rows, pure DB, zero API calls: 1,901 corrected up, **4 corrected DOWN** (`119-hr-1238` 2→1, `119-s-1326` 8→7, `119-s-2904` 24→23, `119-s-3966` 21→20 — right, and a decrease, named in advance so it surprises nobody), and 108 NULL→value. **THIS ENTRY'S OWN MECHANISM WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART.** It says the column drifts because the sync cannot keep it correct; measured at HO 677, the sync's write was never *incorrect* — it writes the LIVE detail-endpoint count at upsert time, and **136 of 136 bills upserted within a day agreed with their roster, 501 of 501 within a week, while 0 of the 1,905 disagreeing bills had been upserted in that window** (oldest `update_date` 2025-02-28). The defect was **frequency, not correctness-at-write** — so the fix that mattered was not a better writer but a **single owner**, which is precisely what this entry lacked. **The NULL rule, ruled explicitly rather than left implicit:** write `active_count` when > 0, **NULL when 0**. The first reason is a measurement — **no bill in this corpus has ever stored `0`** (NULL 3,798 · `=0` 0 · `>0` 13,931), so NULL already IS the zero, and writing `0` would invent a third state and flip the panel's `cosponsor_count != null` gate true on **3,690 bills**, rendering an empty COSPONSORS row nobody asked for. NULL's meaning therefore changes from *the API omitted the key* to *the refresh saw zero active cosponsors*, with the ambiguity it used to carry now resolved in the DATA MODEL instead: `active_count = 0` beside a `checked_at` is a **verified** zero. Held under a real write — still 0 rows storing `0` afterwards. **What this does NOT claim, and the entry claims no more:** the column is now **bounded-stale, not correct.** Its freshness is each bill's last check, and at close **97.1% of state rows still carried the HO 676 SEED stamp** rather than a refresh check — so these are corrections to **2026-08-19/20 truth, not to now**, bounded going forward by the ~18.5-day sweep. Better than unbounded, which is what the column had. *Verified on screen, against HO 675's own captures as the before:* `119-hr-1628` 202/252 → **252/252**, `119-hr-8591` 10/11 → **11/11**, eight agreeing bills unmoved, four genuine-zero bills still hiding the row, and two of the 108 (`119-hr-8411`, `119-hres-1334`) now rendering the row **and their faces**, which they had hidden entirely. *The original entry's text follows, preserved:* **`bills.cosponsor_count` is systematically stale — 13.8% of bills, and always low (opened HO 674).** *Owner:* Code. Now that the roster exists the two can be compared corpus-wide, and they disagree on **1,915 of 13,922 bills (13.8%)**. The direction is the finding: **1,911 have a roster larger than the stored count and only 4 smaller**, with a summed net gap of **+4,887 cosponsors** the column does not know about. *(A 40-bill pre-backfill sample read 30%; the corpus reads 13.8%, so that sample over-estimated by ~2× and the corpus figure supersedes it.)* **Mechanism:** `cosponsor_count` is refreshed only when a bill is re-upserted (`lib/sync.ts:215`), and cosponsors accrue without reliably moving the bill's `updateDate`, so no upsert fires and the count silently drifts behind — every disagreeing bill sampled was last updated in May–June against an August reading. **The conclusion, and it is the point of the entry: once the rosters exist, the roster IS the better source, and `cosponsor_count` becomes a cache of it that nothing refreshes.** HO 674 deliberately did not touch the column (doubling the blast radius of an ingestion HO), and its own cross-check compares the **active** subset only — `pagination.count` excludes withdrawals while the roster stores them. **IT IS NOW ON SCREEN, which is a second and independent reason to take this entry (HO 675).** The expand panel renders the stale column as its cosponsor total and the roster as its party-group headers, so on **1,919 of 13,926 bills (13.78%)** the panel shows a total that does not equal the sum of the groups printed two lines below it — net **+4,880**, delta **median +1 · p75 +3 · p95 +8 · max +50**, with **56 bills off by more than 10**. Ruled visible at HO 675 rather than repaired there: sourcing the total from the roster is exactly the quiet fix that HO would have had to make silently, and the column is this entry's to close. **Captured, not asserted** — `119-hr-8591` (10 vs 11) and `119-hr-1628` (202 vs 252) are in the HO 675 artefacts, and the tail case reads as mildly stale rather than obviously broken because the total is a 12.5px sans line and the group counts are 9.5px mono annotations, so only a reader who does the arithmetic sees it. That is a reason to fix it, not a reason to relax. *Close (three acceptable outcomes, and a reason is one of them):* either the count is **derived** from `bill_cosponsors` (a view, a trigger, or a recompute in the sync), or it is **dropped** and its six readers repointed at the roster, or a **reason is recorded** for keeping a column known to drift — with, in every case, the disagreement rate re-measured afterwards and stated as a fraction. **AMENDED HO 676 — THE PREMISE NOW HOLDS, which is what makes this entry actionable rather than aspirational.** This entry's conclusion — *once the rosters exist, the roster IS the better source, and `cosponsor_count` becomes a cache of it that nothing refreshes* — was true about the roster and false about the refresh until HO 676 shipped one (`2779129`, backlog `:80` struck). A roster that nothing maintained was not a better source than a stale column; it was a differently-stale one. It is now maintained on a `40 */3` cron. **Two corrections to the figures above, both measured at HO 676:** the disagreement reads **1,905 of 13,931** (from 1,915 of 13,922 at HO 674 and 1,919 of 13,926 at HO 675 — it drifts, so treat every one of these as a reading and re-measure), and the **4 bills where the roster is SMALLER than the count are not noise** — they are the column stale ABOVE the truth after a cosponsor withdrew, which HO 676 had to build a trigger clause around because a refresh can lower the roster toward reality but can never raise a wrong count to meet it. That direction is a second drift mechanism this entry's *always low* framing does not cover, and whichever of the three outcomes closes this entry has to handle it.

Integrity at full scale: **cosponsor rows resolve to `members.bioguide_id` at 100.00%** (168,610/168,610 — the party-split UI will resolve every row), related rows resolve to `bills.id` at **99.20%**, and the 82 that do not are their own filed entry below rather than an accepted loss. The original entry's text follows, preserved: **Cosponsor roster + related-bill identities — one ingestion domain, and rulings 2 and 3 of HO 673 are blocked on it (opened HO 673).** *Owner:* Code. Both are the **same shape of gap**, which is why this is one entry and not two: Congress.gov's bill *detail* endpoint returns each as a `{count, url}` stub and never a list, so the corpus has counts and no identities. **Cosponsors:** `bills.cosponsor_count` (INTEGER, `scripts/migrate.ts:1389`) populated from `$.cosponsors.count`; `lib/sync.ts:42` types the API as `cosponsors?: { count?: number }`; `scripts/backfill-cosponsors.ts` is pure SQL over `raw_json` with no API call; **none of the 50 `CREATE TABLE`s is a roster** (control: the `amendments` table grep returns 1, so the instrument discriminates). **Related bills:** zero `relatedBills`/`related_bill` references in tracked code — every "companion" hit is the unrelated *news-row* companion pill — and measured on prod, `$.relatedBills` is present on **6,773 of 17,722 bills**, always `{count, url}`, never a list (control: `$.cosponsors.count` present on 13,922, so the JSON path instrument works). The two sub-resources are `…/bill/{congress}/{type}/{number}/cosponsors` and `…/relatedbills`. **The party split additionally needs a party per cosponsor**, which resolves through the existing `members` join at no new column — but there are no rows to resolve. **What this blocks:** HO 673's ruling 2 (six cosponsor faces, party-split, unused shares redistributed, names on hover) and ruling 3 (related bills format R2, companion promoted into its own bordered line). Their ruling record is `docs/design/mock-673-sponsor-cosponsor.html` and `docs/design/mock-673-related-bills.html`, both committed at HO 673 and both **self-declaring their data as stand-ins**. Nothing was part-built: shipping the markup or CSS ahead of a consumer is the `.pdc*` class HO 668 spent a handoff excising, and HO 664 settled that a comment is not a caller. **Price it before building it** — this is a per-bill sub-resource fetch across 17,722 bills with its own rate-limit, cost and cadence questions, and it should get its own STEP 0 pricing rather than being inherited from this entry's framing. *Close (must be able to fail):* a **named** bill's cosponsor roster and related-bill identities are readable from the local DB **without a network call** — checked by querying that bill by id and printing both lists, which today returns nothing and must return rows.

- ~~**`/api/cron/summarize` flushes the `bills` tag UNCONDITIONALLY every 10 minutes, and 98.7% of those flushes wrote nothing (opened HO 670 — a SITE-WIDE finding the `/welcome` measurement merely surfaced; ruled its own HO on the review).**~~ — **CLOSED HO 671 (`3edbb3d`), guard `if (stats.ok > 0) revalidateTag("bills")`.** The close criterion is answered by measurement, all four readings taken against a real server with the HO 670 rows tap, each able to fail: **A** warm visit **0** (the tap and cache behaving) · **B** idle tick (`summarized: 0`) then a visit **0** — *the guard took* · **C** a real WRITING tick (`summarized: 1`, 1,800 prompt / 122 output tokens, so a genuine Gemini call and a genuine row write) then a visit **89,090**, with the visit after it back to **0** — *the flush still fires on a write and settles* · **D** direct `POST /api/revalidate?tag=bills` then a visit **89,090** — the positive control proving B's zero is a reading, not silence. C and D agreeing to the row is what makes the pair mean something. **The guard shape is `ok > 0`, chosen on column visibility rather than on counts:** the deciding cell (`ok = 0, failed > 0`) was empty across 300 ticks, and the failure path writes only `summarize_failed_at` / `summarize_attempts`, which **0 of the 37 tagged queries read**. **`/api/sync` KEPT its unconditional flush**, ruled at STEP 0 on a discriminator rather than by analogy: its upsert sits **inside** the loop's `try/catch`, so a throw after the row commits is caught and leaves `upserted` at 0 with the write landed — invisible in the return value — where summarize's writes sit **outside** its `catch`, so a partial write cannot reach the flush at all. 4 flushes/day is cheap insurance; 144 was not. **Producing C required one authorised prod write** (Corey, HO 671 review): `119-hr-3370`'s `summary` nulled, full row captured first, the bill picked to be invisible on every `/welcome` surface — introduced stage, last action 2026-02-11, `stage_observed_at` NULL, not among the 12 oldest by `latest_action_date` (the stalls panel's exact slice), 0 news mentions, no cluster. It healed on the same tick: corpus back to **17,716/17,716**, **0** `stage_transitions` rows appended (the predicted `noop`), `text_length` and `stage` identical; `topics` came back **`["government_operations","housing"]`** where it had been **`["other"]`** — the regenerated classification is different, and arguably better, but it is a real content change and is recorded rather than smoothed over. **Cron-lock side effect, named because it is a local action with a prod consequence:** `claimCronLock` is shared state, so the local tick took the slot lock prod's `*/10` would otherwise have claimed and prod's tick no-op'd for that window — the HO 432 design working as intended, harmless, and on the record.

  **The residue is the new steady state, and it is the number the entry existed to produce.** Flushes fall from ~**148/day** (144 unconditional + 4 sync) to ~**6/day** (~2 writing summarize ticks — 4 in 300 ticks over 49.8h — plus sync's 4), a **~25× reduction**, and it lands on every consumer of all **37** `bills`-tagged entries, not just the page that found it. `/welcome` alone goes from ~13.2M rows/day if continuously visited to ~**0.53M**. Nothing was traded for it: no query changed, no panel was dropped, no schedule moved.

  Original entry, kept verbatim below because the close is only legible against what it closed: *Owner:* Code. `app/api/cron/summarize/route.ts:88` calls `revalidateTag("bills")` immediately after `runSummarize`, outside any condition, on a `*/10` schedule — **~144 flushes/day**, plus `/api/sync`'s 4 (`0 */6`), so ~**148**. It invalidates the tag for **every** `bills`-tagged consumer on **every** route, not just the page that found it: **37 cache entries in `lib/queries.ts` carry the tag** (27 `["bills"]` alone, plus 10 in combination with `news-breaking` / `members` / `votes` / `committees`).

  **The write-free share is measured, not inferred** (`scripts/diagnostic/summarize-flush-670.ts`, over `cron_runs`): **300 consecutive ticks**, 2026-08-16T13:40Z → 2026-08-18T15:30Z — **300 reached the flush, 4 summarized ≥1 bill, 296 summarized ZERO (98.7%)**, 0 lock-skips, 0 unreadable payloads. That ratio is the expected consequence of HO 406 working: the queue is drained, inflow is ~30 bills/day, and a `*/10` tick clears 100–120, so the overwhelming majority of ticks correctly find nothing to do — **and flush anyway**. The HO 669 envelope trap was avoided rather than dodged: the raw row is printed (`{ok, elapsedMs, payload:{summarized,…}}`, the real fields one level down) and the control holds — `wrote` reads **4**, not 0, so a zero is a genuine idle tick and not a misread field.

  **Cost, from the `/welcome` measurement in the entry below:** the first visit after a `bills` flush costs **89,090** rows, matching the sum of that page's eight `bills`-tagged entries (**88,987**) to within 103 — the sum-match is what identifies the mechanism rather than merely observing a number. **The coupling that decides the fix: ~94% of `/welcome`'s per-render cost is `bills`-tagged, so the lever on the absolute number is the FLUSH CADENCE, not the dataset count.** Gating the flush on a write would remove **98.7%** of the flushes and cut the page's steady-state bill by roughly an order of magnitude **without touching a single query or dropping a panel**; trimming datasets would not — dropping a whole panel's three datasets saves far less than one avoided flush, and every other `bills`-tagged route gets the same relief for free.

  **The shape of the fix is one line and the precedent is already in the file:** `stats.ok` is in scope at the call site, and the lock-overlap path (`route.ts`, "another drain in flight") **already returns before the flush** — so a tick that does nothing not flushing is the existing behaviour for one case, not a new principle. Also note `revalidateTag("bills")` in `/api/sync` is likewise unconditional, and `runSync` reports `upserted`/`seen`; same question, same file family, decide both in that HO. **Adjacent, comment-only:** the route's own header still says "Schedule lives in vercel.json (13:00 UTC daily…)" while `vercel.json` reads `*/10 * * * *` — stale since the schedule changed; it rides that HO's touch of the file. *Close:* the flush fires only when the tick wrote, **measured** — a tick with `summarized: 0` followed by a page visit costs **0** rows, while a tick that wrote is followed by the full ~89k. *Instrument fails honestly:* if the zero-write tick's next visit still costs ~89k, the guard did not take, and if the writing tick's visit costs 0 the guard is over-tight and the surface is serving stale summaries — both readable from the same two measurements. **Its own HO — do not build it inside a feature handoff.**

Measured 2026-08-19 across a chosen window (`scripts/diagnostic/flush-window-672.ts`, `/welcome` every 20s, `/api/version` sampled in the same cycle as an untagged control): baseline **p25 333 · p50 353 · p75 380 ms**; **sensitivity PASS** at the six-cron 00:00Z slot (peak **2251 ms, 6.4×**); **one clean positive** — the `00:10:20` tick wrote (`summarized=21`), the guard fired, and the next sample read **1304 ms (3.7×)** before returning to 391/350/368; **one clean negative** — the `00:20:20` tick was idle, and the following five samples read 334/348/345/330/307, **entirely inside the baseline IQR**. The control channel held **91–115 ms through the 2251 ms cycle**, so the excursions are server-side regeneration and not network. **Cadence — not a latency question, and already answered better elsewhere:** how often a tick writes is a `cron_runs` claim, measured over 8 days at **3.3 writing ticks/day, mean, range 0–5** — which supersedes the ~2/day this arc had been carrying (HO 671 sampled a 49.8h stretch containing 08-16, which wrote nothing all day, and 08-17, which wrote once; **a point estimate reported without its range invites exactly that correction**). So the guarded cadence is ~**7.3 flushes/day mean, range 4–9**, against ~148 — the conclusion unchanged, the point estimate corrected. A second window at 06:10Z was **declined**: it would buy more negatives for the half the tick log already answers. **THE EXPERIMENT IS THIN AND THE REASON IS SELF-INFLICTED:** `TaskStop` reported success twice without killing the detached children, so three samplers ran concurrently and the load changes at **00:40:01** and **00:50:02** landed on two of the four planned negatives, which were discarded as unattributable (oddities). **One positive, one negative, one sensitivity reading.** **The variance cuts both ways and this hour ran HOT, not cold** — two writing ticks in one hour (`00:01:15` and `00:10:20`, both `summarized=21`) against a 3.3/day mean. **One unplanned corroboration, in a single two-minute stretch:** the idle `00:31:19` summarize tick produced nothing (321 ms), and the news cron flushing `news-breaking` fifteen seconds later produced **977 ms** — the discriminator working in both directions, on two different tags, inside two minutes.

  **The amplification is worse than the review's estimate and the reason is a second cron.** `/api/sync` fires `revalidateTag("bills")` 4×/day (`0 */6`) — but **`/api/cron/summarize` fires it too, UNCONDITIONALLY, on a `*/10` schedule** (`app/api/cron/summarize/route.ts:88`), so the ceiling is ~**148 flushes/day**, not 4. **Yes, the 86400s entries carry it:** `getMembersRanked` is `revalidate 86400, tags ["members","bills","votes"]` and `getFeedBills` is `86400, ["bills","news-breaking"]`, so both are flushed by a tag ~148× more often than their own timer would ever expire — `getMembersRanked` alone is 20,995 × up to 148 ≈ **3.1M rows/day** rather than the ~21k/day its TTL implies. The other two 86400s entries are NOT amplified: `getMostCompetitiveRaces` (`race-ratings`) is flushed weekly (`0 11 * * 3`) and `getLatestMarketTicks` (`markets`) ~24×/weekday by the markets crons at 3,004 each (~72k/day).

  **The STEP 0 table conflicts with this and the STEP 0 table is WRONG — the blank cache column was mine, and an empty cell read as "uncached".** The `86400 · tags` reading is right, and it is right three independent ways: (a) the source, read directly — `lib/queries.ts:6537` `["getMembersRanked"]`, `:6545` `{ revalidate: 86400, tags: ["members","bills","votes"] }`; (b) the warm measurement — visits 2, 4 and 6 each cost **0** rows, which is impossible for an uncached read; (c) the flush measurement — invalidating `bills` makes exactly those entries reappear in the cost, which identifies *which* entries the tag governs instead of just showing that something is cached.

  **The lever is the flush cadence, not the dataset count — stated here because it is the decision this entry exists to inform.** ~94% of the page's per-render cost is `bills`-tagged, so making the summarize flush conditional on a write (filed separately above; 98.7% of its flushes wrote nothing) would cut this page's steady-state bill by roughly an order of magnitude **without dropping a panel or touching a query**, while trimming datasets — the intuitive move — would not: the whole Patterns panel is 4,477 rows against 89,090 per flush. **What actually bounds the bill is `min(visits, flushes)`**, since a flush costs nothing until someone arrives: a continuously-visited page is ~**13.2M rows/day** (148 × 89,090; the old page would be ~8.1M), one visit an hour is ~2.1M/day, and an unvisited hour is free. For scale, HO 582 measured the markets-tape burn at ~17.6M/day per open tab against an ~88M/day plateau, so this is the same order as the item that arc was opened for. *Close:* confirm the shape **on prod against a real cron-driven flush** — a summarize tick followed by a `/welcome` visit, read off the Turso rows-read slope rather than a local tap — **or** record why the local measurement suffices (it uses the same `revalidateTag` primitive the crons call, via the app's own `/api/revalidate` route). *Instrument:* if the mechanism holds, prod shows a step of ~89k rows per visited flush window and **nothing** in unvisited ones; a flat slope across visited windows falsifies it and means the Data Cache behaves differently under Vercel than under `next start`, which would itself be the finding.

It carries **six** instances, and **three are about reading an instrument wrongly rather than about the thing under test**, which is the pattern the entry exists to name: HO 670's green gates over three wrong layouts (*a visual check with no capture is not a check*); HO 671's `| tail -3` showing stack frames instead of the message (*never `tail` a command whose failure mode is a stack trace*); HO 671's same one-liner being a **write** whose failure mode was "unclear whether it landed" (*a write instrument that does not read back is not an instrument*); HO 672's `git check-ignore -v` **exiting 0 on a negated pattern**, so "not ignored" reads as "ignored" — authoritative instrument `git add --dry-run`; HO 672's **`TaskStop` reporting success without killing the detached children**, the first here where a tool lied about an *action* rather than a state; and HO 672's **`--word-diff=color` written to a file**, an export carrying **0 markers in 38,468 bytes** because the markers are ANSI escapes — *an empty word-diff and a diff with no changes are the same bytes*. A clean grep and a genuinely additive commit are the same output. **Its reopening criterion survives the widening verbatim** — if the next render-touching HO's paste carries no capture, the step is decoration and this reopens.

- ~~**14 UNTRACKED files in `scripts/diagnostic/` sit inside `tsconfig.json`'s `include`, so local `typecheck` can red-light on files a fresh clone never sees — local is STRICTER than CI, and that direction is the whole point (opened HO 669).** *Owner:* Code. `tsconfig.json` includes `scripts/**/*.ts` with `strict` + `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, and `exclude` is `["node_modules"]` only. `scripts/diagnostic/` currently holds **129 tracked** files and **14 untracked** ones — the design mocks' sibling habit, and `docs/handoffs/` is gitignored the same way (see the handoffs entry). **Demonstrated live at HO 669, which is the evidence:** the WATCH probe `scripts/diagnostic/sync-lead-watch-669.ts` was written, never staged, and **failed `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck` with two `TS2532` errors** under `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` — a gate on the HO 669 chore went red for a file that is not in the commit, not on the branch, and invisible to CI. **State the DIRECTION, because it decides how much this matters:** the risk here is a **false RED locally**, not a false green in CI. CI clones the repo, so it typechecks only tracked files and cannot be fooled by an untracked probe; the failure mode is local noise that looks like the change under test breaking, and the cost is misattributed debugging time on a chore whose own gates are supposed to be clean. **The tracked half of this class is already recorded and is the opposite hazard** — SKILL `:2332` (HO 485) covers a probe that is *committed* and breaks the Vercel build. This is the untracked half, and it is new: it cuts against the **parity** implied by SKILL § Continuous integration (HO 488), where CI running `typecheck` + `build` reads as a backstop for the same check — the two run over **different file sets** whenever untracked scripts exist locally. **HO 667 hit the tracked side of the same seam** when the 479 diagnostic coupled a deletion to typecheck. *Close:* decide and record ONE of — (a) leave it and document the asymmetry where the pre-push gate is described, so a red local typecheck on an untracked probe is read correctly instead of investigated as a regression; (b) narrow `include`, or add `scripts/diagnostic/**` to `exclude`, and accept that committed diagnostics stop being typechecked (which would undo the HO 485 protection — probably wrong); or (c) keep the coverage and require probes to typecheck, which is today's de-facto rule but has never been written down as one.~~ — **CLOSED HO 672 (`bb746c6`), by (c) plus a fourth option the entry did not list.** Probes now default to **tracked** and `scripts/diagnostic/scratch/` is the gitignored **and** `tsconfig`-excluded opt-in for work known disposable when written — the only place under `scripts/` where a file cannot reach a gate. **Two of this entry's premises were wrong and the corrections are what chose the option:** the count was **27 pre-existing untracked** (29 with this HO's two probes), not 14; and untracked was **never the house pattern** — 126 of 154 diagnostics were already tracked, `scripts/diagnostic/` is in no `.gitignore`, and `git check-ignore` returns nothing, so the 29 were untracked because nobody ran `git add`. That inverts (c) from "require probes to typecheck" into "finish the convention already in force." **Option (b) is refused with the file list as evidence** — excluding the directory blinds **126 tracked** files including every standing instrument SKILL names (`cold-start-audit-332`, `delete-rebuild-erosion-566`, `participation-falsify-595`, `hydration-clock-harness-590`, `layout-audit-606`, 11 more), which is the SKILL `:2332` objection made concrete. **The DIRECTION this entry insisted on stating is now measurable:** CI checks out a fresh clone, so it typechecked **185** `scripts/` files while this box typechecked **214** — that gap *was* the bug. Criterion met **per class**: **invisible → destined-for-CI** for the tracked location, **impossible** for `scratch/`. Reproduction ran both, byte-identical `TS2532`: tracked → `BUILD EXIT=1` + `TYPECHECK EXIT=2` naming the file; scratch → both exit 0 **while the identical defect sat on disk**, which is what proves the exclusion rather than the file's absence is doing the work. **Second finding, closed for free:** six untracked probes were cited as the instrument behind a recorded finding while existing on one machine — SKILL named `welcome-read-budget-670.ts` as the source of the 94,240-row `/welcome` price and a fresh clone could not run it. **One thing this entry could not see about itself:** the exposure fired three times (HO 669/670/671) and **was never visible between firings**, which is why three sessions each read it as a one-off.

  **HO 671 — THIRD live firing, and the first to hit `next build` rather than only `typecheck`.** `scripts/diagnostic/cache-options-670.ts:52` (an untracked HO 670 probe) failed `TS2532` under `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` and red-lit **both** `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck` on a commit the file is not part of — so "just run the build instead" is not a way around this. **The interesting half is when it went red:** that probe was written during HO 670's *review round*, after HO 670's final gate run, so HO 670's gates were green and then the tree went red with nothing to catch it — tracked files were clean, so CI stayed green and the failure was invisible until the next HO's first gate. Firing count now **3** (HO 669's WATCH probe, HO 667's 479 diagnostic coupling, this), all local false-REDs on clean work rather than false-greens in CI. *Close criterion EXTENDED:* whatever lands must make an untracked probe's type error either **impossible or visible** — not merely tolerable. A fix that only silences it (widening `exclude` so probes are never checked) trades a false RED for a real blind spot, since a probe that is later committed then breaks the Vercel build (SKILL `:2332`, HO 485) — so the two halves of the seam have to be closed together.

- ~~**The `bills_agg` sponsor aggregate — the largest single read consumer on the database, untouched (HO 602).**~~ — **RESOLVED BY PROBE, HO 624** (`scripts/diagnostic/bills-agg-cost-624.ts`, on main at `55529d2`). **The 110M / 26% attribution was the PRE-595 statement, not `bills_agg`.** `participationAggCte()` full-scanned `member_votes` *inside the same query text* as `bills_agg`, and the per-query ledger attributes by statement text — so when HO 595 materialized that scan away in July, the dead cost stayed on a trailing 30-day leaderboard read 2026-08-05. **Measured current cost (2026-08-07, 17,463-bill corpus): 20,752 rows per call** for the full `getMembersRanked` statement — component sum 18,617 (`bills_agg` 17,463 + `part_agg` 554 + `members` 553 + `palestine_scorecard` 47), the ~2.1k remainder being ≈4 index seeks per member row across the three LEFT JOINs. That is **16.7× under the ledger's ~347k**, and the coincidence test is decisive: **`|347k − member_votes 365,996| = 18,996`** against **`|347k − measured| = 326,248`**. Plans re-verified covering on both sorts (`SEARCH bills USING COVERING INDEX idx_bills_sponsor_agg`); 14.1ms / 6.2ms, **one sample each**, laptop→us-west-2. **NOT the largest consumer; NOT worth a materialization** — the sketch would add ~1,092 written rows to save a 17k covering scan that reads in single-digit ms. **The congress lever is dead:** all 17,463 sponsored bills are congress 119, so a `congress = 119` clause excludes **zero** rows. *(`includeCeremonial` is not a lever either — both CTE variants read 17,463; the predicate filters rows RETURNED inside the covering scan, not rows read.)* **The probe's own instrument note is worth keeping:** `@libsql/client` v0.14.0 does not surface per-statement `rows_read`, so the probe reads Turso's hrana `/v2/pipeline` directly and *measures* what the ledger meters instead of deriving it — and a **bare `COUNT(*)` under-reports by 227–416×** (366,496 rows → `rows_read: 1617`) because it is answered from B-tree interior pages, so nothing may price a scan by bare count.

  **Variant-surface inventory** — the ledger groups by statement text, so these are **THREE separate lines**; confirm which one any future dashboard read is showing before attributing it: `getMembersRanked` (`queries.ts:6283`, key = filters × sort × page × pageSize — 72 combinations before state/q/page, × 56 states) · `getCommitteeRoster` (`queries.ts:6427`, per committee — 236 × 3 sorts × 2 = 1,416) · `searchMembers` (`queries.ts:7583`, per query string at a 600s TTL, **unbounded by construction**).

  **THE REUSABLE LESSON — date every per-query ledger read against the fixes that landed inside its window.** A killed statement stays on the leaderboard for the length of the window: a 30-day view read 2026-08-05 still contained weeks of executions from a query shape that stopped existing in July, ranked by a cost nothing had paid since. This is the HO 420/577 invalidation class at ledger scale, and it is the measured-number rule one level up — a ledger line is a measurement of `f(query, corpus, window)` being read as a property of the query.
